1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward, where it exhibits Goodhart effects (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); and cross-domain generalization across logic types and dataset families (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
5	A hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ (ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ extends to $\{45,46\}$; Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}) on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline uses negative log-likelihood on gold formalizations.\footnote{Seeds control initialization and rollout sampling; all conditions share the same fixed training subset.}
10	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples with greedy decoding;\footnote{Seed~43 uses nucleus sampling ($T{=}0.7$) due to greedy-mode degeneration; excluding s43 yields consistent or stronger collapse: at 1000 steps, both remaining seeds show ${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking (Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}).} all cross-run ISR values are recomputed uniformly by a single evaluator so training-log ISR is never compared across runs.
11	Cross-dataset experiments (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}) use ProofFOL, a silver-standard FOL corpus of ${\sim}$500 training examples constructed by translating ProofWriter~\citep{proofwriter} problems via GPT-4o and filtering through Prover9 (${\sim}70\%$ retention); \emph{stable convergence} denotes $\overline{\text{PR}}_{\text{final\,50}} > 0.90$.
12	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for CE/TS plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate, an upper bound on genuine reasoning: the residual matching none of the eight cataloged patterns (classifier recall is unknown).
13	
14	\paragraph{Statistical considerations.}
15	With three seeds exhibiting multi-attractor dynamics, per-seed qualitative analysis is the primary framework; the divergence itself is a finding.
16	Pooled statistics (CMH) serve as supplementary reference; CMH treats within-seed samples as independent observations from a single trained model, so the true replication count is $N{=}3$ seeds.
17	ProofFOL results ($p{=}0.10$, $N{=}3$) are exploratory.
18	
19	\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}
20	\label{sec:diagnostic}
21	
22	\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}
23	Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42) at 100, 500, 1000 steps.
24	PR rises $32\%\to93\%\to96\%$, a trajectory that execution-only evaluation would interpret as successful optimization; $\text{ISR}^*$ tells the opposite story, $0.642\to0.132\to0.003$ (214$\times$ collapse). %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42
25	By 500 steps, 87.1\% of proved samples embed the conclusion (CE); by 1000 steps, component count inflates $2.8\times$ ($6.58{\to}18.55$) while ISR decreases $214\times$, ruling out shorter formalizations as a mechanical explanation of the collapse.
26	An $O(1)$ binary variant testing only collective premise necessity returns $\text{ISR}{=}1.0$ for all 402 proved samples, confirming that per-component LOO testing is essential for detecting the collapse (Appendix~\ref{app:o1-ablation}).
27	
28	\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}
29	Three seeds diverge radically ($\text{PR}\,\sigma{=}47$\,pp) into conclusion embedding (s42, CE$=$87\%), repetition collapse (s43), and tautological collapse (s44, TS$=$100\%), all converging to ${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking by 1000 steps (Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}; Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44; plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan001-exec-only-s43-1000-r7, plan001-exec-only-s44-1000-r2
30	
31	\textbf{SFT baseline.}
32	SFT on gold formalizations reaches $92.3\%$ non-hacking ($\text{PR}{=}13\%$, 3-seed pooled; $\text{OR}{=}147$ vs.\ exec-only, $p{<}0.001$).
33	SFT serves as a pre-RL reference (not a PR-matched control; its low PR reflects minimal optimization pressure, so hacking-rate differences are confounded with PR level): the base model has zero FOL capability ($\text{PR}{=}0\%$) and SFT's $8\%$ hacking confirms these modes are absent before RL.
34	Supplementary evidence comes from the 100-step comparison (seed~42, $n{=}32$ proved): at $\text{PR}{=}32\%$, hacking already reaches $31\%$ ($9$ TS $+$ $1$ CE / $32$ proved) vs.\ SFT's $8\%$ at comparable PR, consistent with hacking as an RL optimization product rather than a mechanical consequence of prove-rate level (Appendix~\ref{app:100step}). %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3
35	
36	\subsection{Goodhart Effects on ISR-as-Reward}
37	\label{sec:goodhart}
38	
39	We reverse ISR's role from observer to reward: $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, with matched random control ($u\sim\mathcal{U}(0,1)$).
40	The compositional Goodhart effect operates at the level of ISR's LOO definition: models learn to generate formalizations where each component is individually necessary for proof mechanics, without ensuring meaningful reasoning.
41	ISR decline indicates structural change; the mode breakdown (Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}) characterizes this change as degeneration.
42	This is distinct from simple proxy failure. The qualitative shift in hacking modes (TS $0\%{\to}71\%$ under ISR, per-seed direction consistent) demonstrates active optimization against the LOO metric; deconfounding shows the apparent $3.2\times$ ISR advantage collapses to $1.06\times$ after controlling for premise count.
43	
44	\paragraph{Multiplicative coupling.}
45	The reward $r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1{+}\beta\,\text{ISR})$ couples signals; exec-only's $0\%$ non-hacking, the qualitative TS shift ($0\%{\to}71\%$), and an additive variant's PR collapse (Appendix~\ref{app:setup}) constrain this confound.
46	ISR scores are bimodal (near $0$ or ${\geq}0.5$) while random is approximately uniform. A shuffled-ISR control (batch-internal random permutation preserving ISR's bimodal distribution while breaking the sample-level semantic link) yields $\text{PR}{=}15.3{\pm}0.6\%$ with $87.0\%$ non-hacking ($N{=}46$ proved, 3-seed pooled; per-seed $n{\approx}15$ limits statistical power), comparable to SFT ($\text{PR}{=}13\%$, $92\%$ non-hacking) and far below the TS-dominated ISR regime ($71\%$ TS). This rules out distributional shape as the Goodhart driver: the semantic correspondence between ISR and sample quality, not merely ISR's bimodal distribution, is necessary for the TS gaming effect.
47

72	\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$): per-seed consistent gaming.}
73	ISR reward increases TS rates in all three initial seeds (s42--44) relative to matched random: $70.6\%$ ($163/231$) vs.\ $13.7\%$ ($36/262$) pooled (supplementary CMH: $\text{OR}_{\text{CMH}}{=}2.46$, $p{=}5.6{\times}10^{-16}$; $N{=}3$ seed-level units, sample-level $p$ reflects within-seed effect; Appendix~\ref{app:statistical-details}). %% registry: plan002 vs plan018 all seeds; D095 FINAL
74	Random TS is seed-dependent ($38.3\%$ in s43 vs.\ $0\%$ in s42/s44); ISR amplifies TS ($2/3$ seeds at $97$--$100\%$; Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}; 5-seed extension below).
75	TS (a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by construction; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}) is the canonical Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly.
76	Total conclusion embedding ($\text{CE}+\text{TS}$) is $68$--$100\%$ across both conditions; the composition differs: CE dominates under random ($71\%$), TS under ISR. ISR converts CE into TS rather than reducing total embedding (Figure~\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).
77	Random yields higher PR ($87{\pm}8\%$ vs.\ $77{\pm}14\%$): noise disrupts convergence toward a single collapse trajectory.

79	\textbf{Premise-count confound.}
80	The sole non-TS ISR seed (s42) shows raw $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\times$ random ($0.131$), a premise-minimization artifact ($n{=}5.58$ vs.\ $8.46$); matched comparison gives $1.06\times$ (Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis
81	After reclassifying PG and TS false negatives, non-hacking rates ($6.1\%$ ISR vs.\ $9.5\%$ random) are not significant ($p{=}0.182$; Appendix~\ref{app:h8}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test
82	The key asymmetry is compositional: ISR amplifies TS convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\geq}97\%$), while random remains seed-dependent and CE-dominated.
83	
84	\textbf{Higher doses and temporal dynamics.}
85	ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ non-hacking is transient: seed~42 drops $17.3\%{\to}2.2\%$ by 1000 steps as TS escalates to $96.7\%$ (Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}). %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42
86	At higher doses ($\beta{\geq}0.2$), TS becomes universal ($0\%$ non-hacking at $\beta{=}0.2$); $\beta{=}0.3$ destabilizes both ISR ($\text{PR}{=}66{\pm}42\%$) and random ($54{\pm}35\%$), with ISR producing multi-modal failure~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} and random converging to CE ($91\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:beta03}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b02-s42/s43/s44, plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44

90	This bimodal outcome (3/5 seeds dominated by gaming, 2/5 producing substantial genuine reasoning) reinforces the cross-seed instability documented in \S\ref{sec:diagnostic} and underscores that ISR reward outcomes are seed-dependent rather than deterministic. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s45, plan002-additive-b01-s46
91	
92	\subsection{Cross-Domain and Cross-Dataset Evidence}
93	\label{sec:crossdomain}
94	\label{sec:crosslogic}
95	
96	\begin{figure}[t]
97	\centering
98	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}
99	\caption{Cross-logic results. \textbf{Top}: FOL vs.\ Prolog exec-only $\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$; $^\dagger$s43 FOL uses sampling $T{=}0.7$, not strictly comparable). \textbf{Bottom}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition; all 3 seeds reach 0\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}
100	\label{fig:prolog-comparison}
101	\end{figure}
102	
103	\textbf{Prolog: universal Goodhart.}
104	We replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog (Figure~\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).
105	Exec-only seeds show $\text{ISR}^*{\approx}0$ via redundant ground-fact coding (2/3 seeds); ISR flags this mode as it flags FOL's conclusion embedding.
106	Under ISR-as-reward, 3/3 seeds reach 0\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms: clean TS (s42), uppercase pseudo-Prolog bypassing text-match classifiers (s43, all 38 proved at $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$), and query-as-fact gaming (s44, 95/97 at $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$; Table~\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}).
107	Prolog's simpler semantics broadens the gaming basin, eliminating the non-hacking residual entirely.
108	
109	\textbf{ProofFOL exec-only: cross-dataset evidence for structural degeneration, with differing mode distributions.}
110	Two converged seeds ($\text{ISR}^*{<}0.07$) show ${\leq}1.4\%$ genuine; s42 exhibits 100\% TS (absent from all FOLIO exec-only seeds), showing hacking-mode repertoire is dataset-specific ($\text{ISR}^*{<}0.12$ across all seeds, far below baseline $0.31$).
111	
112	\textbf{ProofFOL ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$): beneficial instability (exploratory).}
113	ISR reduces stable convergence from 2/3 to 0/3 seeds, yet ISR seeds average 76.8\% genuine versus 17.4\% for exec-only ($p{=}0.10$, $N{=}3$; Table~\ref{tab:prooffol-detailed}).
114	ProofFOL ``genuine'' excludes only TS and CE (the two modes detectable by text-match classifiers), unlike FOLIO's eight-mode residual; the rates are therefore not directly comparable.
115	However, ISR-condition prove rates ($0.35$--$0.49$) remain far below exec-only (${\sim}1.0$), so apparent beneficial instability may reflect insufficient training rather than a genuine ISR effect.
116	
117	\textbf{Mechanism.}
118	On ProofFOL, hacking yields $\text{ISR}{=}0.0$ while genuine proofs yield $\text{ISR}{>}0.2$; ISR correctly penalizes shortcuts.
119	On FOLIO, $P{\to}P$ yields $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$ (regressional Goodhart~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}), so ISR erroneously rewards tautological hacking.
120	The dataset-dependent Goodhart effect follows from the interaction between LOO granularity and hacking geometry: on FOLIO, the dominant shortcut ($P{\to}P$) produces 2-component formalizations where both are individually LOO-necessary ($\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$); on ProofFOL, hacking embeds the conclusion among many premises (mean $n{=}18.2$), making most individually redundant ($\text{ISR}{\approx}0$).
121	ISR-as-reward thus inherits the dataset's hacking topology: it rewards shortcuts only when the shortcut geometry satisfies the LOO criterion.
122

and cross-domain generalization across logic types and dataset families (\\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}).\nA hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B~\\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ (ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ extends to $\\{45,46\\}$; \\emph{stable convergence} denotes $\\overline{\\text{PR}}_{\\text{final\\,50}} > 0.90$.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for CE/TS plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution; 1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}
5	LLM-solver pipelines~\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15, deepseek-r1} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for component necessity.
6	AlphaProof~\citep{alphaproof2025} applies RL-based formal verification at scale in Lean~4; STA-style diagnostics could probe whether similar structural degeneration occurs in theorem-proving settings.
7	We use FOLIO~\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.
8	
9	\paragraph{Process rewards and reward shaping.}
10	Reward shaping theory~\citep{ng1999policy} establishes conditions under which auxiliary rewards preserve optimal policies; early work applied verifier-based training to mathematical reasoning~\citep{cobbe2021training}, with process-level feedback showing promise over outcome-only signals~\citep{uesato2022solving}.
11	Learned PRMs~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} and verifier-guided search~\citep{nlproofs, vstar} provide step-level feedback but suffer from reward-model noise and distribution shift~\citep{processbench}; \citet{easy-to-hard} show that verifiers trained on easy tasks can generalize to hard ones, though structural gaming remains unaddressed.
12	\citet{gao-effective-rl-reward} show that auxiliary process/outcome rewards can \emph{hurt} RL training relative to success-only reward, a finding our ISR-as-reward results corroborate; \citet{setlur2025rewarding} further demonstrate that reward bonus magnitude must be carefully calibrated, echoing our $\beta$-dependent Goodhart transitions.
13	Symbolic feedback approaches~\citep{rlsf} use solver certificates as token-level rewards, achieving gains on program synthesis; ISR differs in operating at the component level (LOO ablation) rather than the token level, and our results show this structural signal is gamed when optimized.
14	
15	\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}
16	Goodhart's Law is established theoretically~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RLHF and frontier models~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization, bondarenko-spec-gaming, kim-game-formalization}.
17	\citet{kim-game-formalization} document inference-time gaming in logical formalization on FOLIO using frontier models with Lean~4.
18	Our work is complementary: training-time (vs.\ inference), component necessity (vs.\ faithfulness), medium-model/Prover9 (vs.\ frontier/Lean~4); combining STA with inference-time faithfulness detection would provide complete gaming coverage.
19	\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes \emph{catastrophic} Goodhart where KL regularization provably fails, offering a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent the ISR collapse we observe.
20	Recent work further documents spurious reward signals in RLVR~\citep{spurious-rewards, yan2026spurious}, training instability via objective-level hacking~\citep{dong2026probing}, reward hacking in frontier models~\citep{metr-reward-hacking}, and proposes verifiable composite or process rewards to mitigate outcome-only limitations~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}.
21	\citet{helff2026gaming} and \citet{khalifa2026countdown} document verification gaming and provide RLVR hacking testbeds; STA is complementary, offering a structural diagnostic and eight-mode taxonomy for neurosymbolic settings where gaming \emph{emerges during training}.
22	The Goodhart effect follows standard taxonomy~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: regressional on FOLIO (tautologies satisfy the LOO metric), extremal at high $\beta$. Its application to a structural diagnostic designed to \emph{detect} gaming is, to our knowledge, novel; the dataset-dependent asymmetry (the same metric gamed on one dataset, beneficial on another) is a non-trivial empirical finding not predicted by the taxonomy alone.
23	
24	\paragraph{Mutation testing.}
25	STA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; novelty lies in application and findings, not in the perturbation primitive.
26	
```

```text
1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B); RLVR gains from random or incorrect rewards have been shown to be Qwen-specific~\citep{spurious-rewards}, so the structural degeneration patterns we document may not transfer identically to other model families.
5	Cross-dataset evidence (ProofFOL, Prolog) supports generality, but LLaMA-3.2-3B achieves only $\text{PR}{=}16\%$ after SFT warmstart with GRPO unable to amplify beyond this baseline (Appendix~\ref{app:llama-coldstart}), establishing a capability threshold that limits STA's applicability.
6	Three seeds demonstrate qualitative divergence but limit statistical power ($N{=}3$ independent replications); all pooled statistics treat within-seed samples as independent, and the ProofFOL genuine-rate advantage ($p{=}0.10$) is an observation, not a confirmed effect.
7	At our scale (500 training / 100 evaluation examples), non-hacking rates of ${\sim}5\%$ correspond to ${\sim}5$ samples, limiting mode-level precision.
8	ISR quantifies syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness ($\rho{=}{-}0.049$ with NLI accuracy; Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}); its diagnostic value is realized through the mode breakdown, not the scalar alone.
9	
```

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: warning] R23 Action Matrix 独立审查——含 1 个 power analysis 数值错误需修正

## 审查报告 — R23 Action Matrix

**结论**: WARNING（MF2 power analysis 数值有误，其余方案可行）

---

### Q1: MF1 拆分策略是否足够？

**足够，保留 "compositional Goodhart effect" 术语是正确的。** 理由：

1. Manheim & Garrabrant (2019) 的 regressional Goodhart 定义明确覆盖这个现象：proxy 在训练分布上与目标相关，但在优化下偏离。ISR 作为 reward 时，模型学会满足 LOO 定义（TS: ISR≥0.5）而非真实推理——这就是 regressional Goodhart 的教科书实例。
2. 拆分为两个 sub-finding 各自带 p 值（composition shift p<10⁻¹⁵; prevalence unchanged p=0.182）是最诚实的呈现方式。
3. 去掉统一术语会丢失贡献：reviewer 的问题不是"这不是 Goodhart"，而是"两个子发现的显著性不同需要明确区分"。

**唯一风险**：reviewer 可能认为 "compositional shift without prevalence reduction" 贡献不足。应对：在 rebuttal 中强调 C1（诊断框架）是主贡献，C2（Goodhart）是次要贡献。

### Q2: MF2 power analysis — [severity: warning] 数值有误

**d≥2.0 是错的。** 我用 scipy 做了精确计算（N=3 per group, two-sample t-test, α=0.05 two-tailed）：

| Effect size d | Power |
|---|---|
| 2.0 | **0.463** (< 50%) |
| 2.5 | 0.636 |
| 3.0 | **0.783** (~80%) |
| 3.5 | 0.886 |

**正确措辞应为 d≥3.0**（80% power）。建议修改为：

"With $N{=}3$ seeds per condition, seed-level tests achieve 80\% power only for very large effects ($d{\geq}3.0$, Cohen's convention: $d{>}0.8$ is `large'); non-significant comparisons (e.g., prevalence $p{=}0.182$) are exploratory."

或更简洁："$N{=}3$ seeds provide adequate power only for very large effects; non-significant comparisons are exploratory."（不报具体 d 值，避免审稿人质疑计算）

### Q3: SF9 压缩分布

从当前 16:33 编译 PDF 看，主文本溢出约 **4 行**到第 9 页（§5 practitioner advice 段尾 L649-652），不是 15 行。但如果 MF1/MF2 等修改会增加少量文本，预留 15 行 buffer 是合理的。

压缩分布建议调整优先级：

| 位置 | 建议压缩量 | 理由 |
|---|---|---|
| §4.4 cross-domain (L109-121) | **5-6 行** | ProofFOL "beneficial instability (exploratory)" 段可大幅压缩——已标 exploratory 且 PR 远低于 exec-only，"may reflect insufficient training" 的 caveat 可以一句话带过而非四句 |
| §4.5 taxonomy (L592-605) | **3-4 行** | CE/TS/PG 三大模式保留，其余五个模式仅在 Table 2 + Appendix D 呈现，正文"Three modes account for >90%"后直接引用 appendix |
| Intro contributions (C1/C2) | **2-3 行** | C2 可压缩：去掉 "per-seed consistent TS amplification" 等中间细节，只保留结论 |
| §5 Discussion/Conclusion | **2-3 行** | 已经很紧凑，只能微压 |
| §4.3 Goodhart 机制 | **1-2 行** | 如果 MF1 拆分增加文本，从 "multiplicative coupling" 段回收 |

**最激进压缩候选**：§4.4 ProofFOL beneficial instability（L112-116, 5 行），因为论文自己说"may reflect insufficient training rather than a genuine ISR effect"——这段话基本在承认证据不足，可以压成一句放 appendix。

### Q4: SF5 排除 s43 放 Appendix

**Appendix 足够。** 理由：
1. s43 异常是已知且已标注的（footnote 4），审稿人的顾虑是"缺数据支撑叙述"，不是"这个问题需要在正文解决"
2. 正文加一句 "Excluding s43 does not change qualitative conclusions (Appendix Table~X)" 即可
3. 2-seed pooled 的定性结论不变——我在上一轮审查中已验证：exec-only s42(CE=87%) + s44(TS=100%) 均退化；ISR β=0.1 s42(TS=97%) + s44(TS=100%) 均 gaming。去掉 s43 只是减少了一个 seed 的 mode diversity 展示

### Q5: 总体优先级

**排序正确，但建议微调**：

1. **SF9 + MF1 合并做**（HIGH）：压缩文本的同时做 Goodhart reframing，避免来回修改。先压后加。
2. **MF2**（HIGH）：一句话，修改代价极低，但 d 值必须改正。
3. **SF5 + SF6**（MEDIUM）：可以一次性做——s43 sensitivity table + premise-count-matched 行加入 Table 1。
4. **SF7**（MEDIUM）：Helff et al. IPT 比较 2-3 句即可。注意：需要先确认 "helff2026ipt" 是否是一个独立于已有 helff2026gaming (arXiv:2604.15149) 的不同论文。如果是同一篇论文的不同称呼，不需要新 bib 条目。
5. **SF1-SF4, SF8, SF10**（LOW）：文本微调，夹在其他修改中顺便做。

### 遗漏检查

1. **Dong et al. (2602.01103) 已在编译 PDF 的 references 中**（L723-727），说明 Agent 已经开始执行之前的 action matrix。确认这个引用是正确的——论文真实存在，内容关于 RLVR objective-level hacking in MoE。

2. **kimina-prover 仍是 dangling bib entry**：在 references.bib L394 但未被任何 .tex 文件引用。之前在 limitations.tex 中与 alphaproof 一起引用，最近修订中被移除。应清理 bib 或恢复引用。低优先级。

3. **Limitations 长度问题**与 SF9 相关：如果需要压缩主文本，可以考虑也压缩 limitations（当前 ~11 个点，用户规则要求 3 个），但 limitations 不计入 8 页所以这不是页数问题，而是风格问题。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_013SkrR47XvVtyv85cxDLMoA`</summary>

```text
109	\textbf{ProofFOL exec-only: cross-dataset evidence for structural degeneration, with differing mode distributions.}
110	Two converged seeds ($\text{ISR}^*{<}0.07$) show ${\leq}1.4\%$ genuine; s42 exhibits 100\% TS (absent from all FOLIO exec-only seeds), showing hacking-mode repertoire is dataset-specific ($\text{ISR}^*{<}0.12$ across all seeds, far below baseline $0.31$).
111	
112	\textbf{ProofFOL ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$): beneficial instability (exploratory).}
113	ISR reduces stable convergence from 2/3 to 0/3 seeds, yet ISR seeds average 76.8\% genuine versus 17.4\% for exec-only ($p{=}0.10$, $N{=}3$; Table~\ref{tab:prooffol-detailed}).
114	ProofFOL ``genuine'' excludes only TS and CE (the two modes detectable by text-match classifiers), unlike FOLIO's eight-mode residual; the rates are therefore not directly comparable.
115	However, ISR-condition prove rates ($0.35$--$0.49$) remain far below exec-only (${\sim}1.0$), so apparent beneficial instability may reflect insufficient training rather than a genuine ISR effect.
116	
117	\textbf{Mechanism.}
118	On ProofFOL, hacking yields $\text{ISR}{=}0.0$ while genuine proofs yield $\text{ISR}{>}0.2$; ISR correctly penalizes shortcuts.
119	On FOLIO, $P{\to}P$ yields $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$ (regressional Goodhart~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}), so ISR erroneously rewards tautological hacking.
120	The dataset-dependent Goodhart effect follows from the interaction between LOO granularity and hacking geometry: on FOLIO, the dominant shortcut ($P{\to}P$) produces 2-component formalizations where both are individually LOO-necessary ($\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$); on ProofFOL, hacking embeds the conclusion among many premises (mean $n{=}18.2$), making most individually redundant ($\text{ISR}{\approx}0$).
121	ISR-as-reward thus inherits the dataset's hacking topology: it rewards shortcuts only when the shortcut geometry satisfies the LOO criterion.
122	
123	\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}
```

  "old_string": "\\textbf{Mechanism.}\nOn ProofFOL, hacking yields $\\text{ISR}{=}0.0$ while genuine proofs yield $\\text{ISR}{>}0.2$; ISR correctly penalizes shortcuts.\nOn FOLIO, $P{\\to}P$ yields $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ (regressional Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}), so ISR erroneously rewards tautological hacking.\nThe dataset-dependent Goodhart effect follows from the interaction between LOO granularity and hacking geometry: on FOLIO, the dominant shortcut ($P{\\to}P$) produces 2-component formalizations where both are individually LOO-necessary ($\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$); on ProofFOL, hacking embeds the conclusion among many premises (mean $n{=}18.2$), making most individually redundant ($\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0$).\nISR-as-reward thus inherits the dataset's hacking topology: it rewards shortcuts only when the shortcut geometry satisfies the LOO criterion.",
  "new_string": "\\textbf{Mechanism.}\nThe dataset-dependent outcome follows from hacking geometry: on FOLIO, $P{\\to}P$ produces 2-component formalizations where both are LOO-necessary ($\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, regressional Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}); on ProofFOL, hacking embeds conclusions among many premises ($\\bar{n}{=}18.2$), making most individually redundant ($\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0$).\nISR-as-reward thus inherits the dataset's hacking topology: it rewards shortcuts only when the shortcut geometry satisfies the LOO criterion."
}

    "oldString": "\\textbf{Mechanism.}\nOn ProofFOL, hacking yields $\\text{ISR}{=}0.0$ while genuine proofs yield $\\text{ISR}{>}0.2$; ISR correctly penalizes shortcuts.\nOn FOLIO, $P{\\to}P$ yields $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ (regressional Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}), so ISR erroneously rewards tautological hacking.\nThe dataset-dependent Goodhart effect follows from the interaction between LOO granularity and hacking geometry: on FOLIO, the dominant shortcut ($P{\\to}P$) produces 2-component formalizations where both are individually LOO-necessary ($\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$); on ProofFOL, hacking embeds the conclusion among many premises (mean $n{=}18.2$), making most individually redundant ($\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0$).\nISR-as-reward thus inherits the dataset's hacking topology: it rewards shortcuts only when the shortcut geometry satisfies the LOO criterion.",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012j6wevydAR6N1HzchnNL38`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	We study structural degeneration in neurosymbolic reasoning trained with GRPO: models achieve high prove rates while most outputs embed the conclusion among premises---a degenerate shortcut invisible to execution reward.
3	We introduce \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic applying leave-one-out necessity testing to solver inputs; the fraction of necessary components yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR), whose diagnostic value is realized through mode-level analysis.
4	As a diagnostic, component-necessity testing reveals a collapse trajectory hidden from prove rate: ISR falls to near-zero over training while prove rate rises, with each seed converging to a qualitatively different hacking mode.
5	A supervised fine-tuning baseline corroborates that the degradation is GRPO-specific.
6	When repurposed as a training reward, ISR exhibits a compositional Goodhart effect: models shift from conclusion embedding to tautological shortcutting without reducing overall hacking prevalence ($p{=}0.182$); a shuffled-ISR control confirms the shift targets semantic content rather than distributional artifacts.
7	Cross-logic (Prolog) and cross-dataset (ProofFOL) evidence confirms structural degeneration generalizes, documenting ISR's core asymmetry: reliable as a diagnostic, dataset-dependent as an optimization target.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
8	\end{abstract}
9	
```

</details>

      "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nWe study structural degeneration in neurosymbolic reasoning trained with GRPO: models achieve high prove rates while most outputs embed the conclusion among premises---a degenerate shortcut invisible to execution reward.\nWe introduce \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic applying leave-one-out necessity testing to solver inputs; the fraction of necessary components yields the \\emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR), whose diagnostic value is realized through mode-level analysis.\nAs a diagnostic, component-necessity testing reveals a collapse trajectory hidden from prove rate: ISR falls to near-zero over training while prove rate rises, with each seed converging to a qualitatively different hacking mode.\nA supervised fine-tuning baseline corroborates that the degradation is GRPO-specific.\nWhen repurposed as a training reward, ISR exhibits a compositional Goodhart effect: models shift from conclusion embedding to tautological shortcutting without reducing overall hacking prevalence ($p{=}0.182$); a shuffled-ISR control confirms the shift targets semantic content rather than distributional artifacts.\nCross-logic (Prolog) and cross-dataset (ProofFOL) evidence confirms structural degeneration generalizes, documenting ISR's core asymmetry: reliable as a diagnostic, dataset-dependent as an optimization target.\\footnote{Code and data: \\url{[anonymized]}}\n\\end{abstract}\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01WpyRdHqbmDq4GzwqNLunxP`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	A GRPO-trained formalizer achieves a 93\% prove rate on first-order logic problems, yet 87\% of its proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises.
5	The solver succeeds not because the model has learned to reason, but because the formalization trivially entails any conclusion already appearing as a premise.
6	This structural shortcut (an instance of shortcut learning~\citep{geirhos2020shortcut} in the neurosymbolic setting) is invisible to execution-based evaluation: prove rate registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation or degenerate circular embedding.
7	
8	\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}
9	Prove rate, the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}, conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.
10	Recent work applies GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on component necessity.
11	Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.
12	A separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \emph{diagnostic} for component necessity in neurosymbolic outputs.
13	
14	\paragraph{Component necessity testing.}
15	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic applying \emph{component necessity testing}, leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs, to quantify component necessity.
16	For each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR), with $\text{ISR}{=}1$ indicating full necessity and $\text{ISR}{=}0$ complete redundancy.
17	LOO ablation itself is standard~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; our contribution is the empirical discovery that the same LOO structure making ISR informative as a diagnostic also makes it vulnerable to gaming as a reward, a compositional Goodhart effect on structural metrics.
18	ISR captures component necessity rather than semantic faithfulness (downstream NLI: $\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$).
19	
20	\begin{figure}[t]
21	\centering
22	\includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{figures/fig5_training_isr_curves.pdf}
23	\caption{ISR training trajectories across reward conditions (proved samples only, rolling $w{=}20$). Exec-only collapses to $\text{ISR}{\approx}0$, confirming structural degeneration (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); the ${\sim}0.5$ plateau is an $n{=}2$ artifact. ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ seeds rise toward $\text{ISR}{\approx}1$ via TS gaming (Goodhart effect, \S\ref{sec:goodhart}); random remains low.}
24	\label{fig:training-isr-curves}
25	\end{figure}
26	
27	\paragraph{ISR as diagnostic.}
28	As a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\citep{folio}, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR (Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}): for seed~42, $\text{ISR}^{*}{:}\,0.642\to 0.132\to 0.003$ over 100--1000 steps (99.5\% degradation; all 3 seeds: ${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking by 1000 steps) while PR rises $32\%{\to}96\%$.
29	Three seeds diverge radically (PR $\sigma{=}47$\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different mode (conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse), yet all reach ${\geq}93\%$ structural hacking by 1000 steps.
30	An SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\text{PR}{=}13.0\%$ with 92.3\% non-hacking (upper bound; 3-seed pooled, 1000 steps), consistent with GRPO-specific degradation rather than data or capacity limitations.
31	
32	\paragraph{Goodhart effects.}
33	Repurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, ISR produces a compositional Goodhart effect~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}.
34	On FOLIO, this instantiates \emph{regressional} Goodhart~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: $P{\to}P$ tautologies yield $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$, so ISR rewards the shortcut; at $\beta{=}0.1$, ISR increases TS rates in 3 of 5 seeds (70.6\% vs.\ 13.7\% random pooled; two seeds retain ${\sim}55\%$ non-hacking), but the sole apparent ISR advantage reduces to $1.06\times$ after controlling for premise count.
35	At $\beta{\geq}0.2$, \emph{extremal} Goodhart dominates ($\text{PR}{=}66{\pm}42\%$ ISR, $54{\pm}35\%$ random at $\beta{=}0.3$).
36	On ProofFOL, hacking yields $\text{ISR}{=}0.0$, so ISR correctly penalizes shortcuts, associated with beneficial instability (76.8\% vs.\ 17.4\% genuine under a coarser classifier, $p{=}0.10$).
37	ISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \emph{reliable as observer, dataset-dependent as optimizer.}
38	
39	\paragraph{Contributions.}
40	In this case study (Qwen3-4B, 6 reward conditions, 3--5 seeds, 2 logic types, 2 dataset families, up to 1000 steps), we contribute: (\textbf{C1}) STA as a diagnostic framework (ISR metric, eight-mode hacking taxonomy, and cross-domain evaluation protocol) revealing structural collapse invisible to PR (${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking, 3 seeds, 1000 steps; 99.5\% in seed~42), with an SFT control consistent with GRPO-specific degradation (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\textbf{C2}) a compositional Goodhart effect on ISR-as-reward: TS amplification without significant prevalence reduction ($p{=}0.182$; apparent ISR advantage collapses to $1.06\times$ after deconfounding; shuffled-ISR control rules out distributional shape; \S\ref{sec:goodhart}), with cross-domain evidence: confirmed in Prolog (3/3 seeds 0\% non-hacking) and exploratory in ProofFOL ($p{=}0.10$), showing dataset-dependent outcomes (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
41	These are cautionary results from a Qwen3-4B case study, replicated across dataset families, relevant to any work treating a structural proxy as a training signal (generalization scope discussed in \S\ref{sec:limitations}).
42	
```

    "oldString": "On FOLIO, this instantiates \\emph{regressional} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: $P{\\to}P$ tautologies yield $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, so ISR rewards the shortcut; at $\\beta{=}0.1$, ISR increases TS rates in 3 of 5 seeds (70.6\\% vs.\\ 13.7\\% random pooled; two seeds retain ${\\sim}55\\%$ non-hacking), but the sole apparent ISR advantage reduces to $1.06\\times$ after controlling for premise count.",
    "newString": "On FOLIO, this instantiates \\emph{regressional} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: $P{\\to}P$ tautologies yield $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, so ISR rewards the shortcut; at $\\beta{=}0.1$, ISR significantly shifts hacking composition from CE to TS ($p{<}10^{-15}$; 3 of 5 seeds) without significantly reducing prevalence ($p{=}0.182$); the sole apparent ISR advantage reduces to $1.06\\times$ after controlling for premise count.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nA GRPO-trained formalizer achieves a 93\\% prove rate on first-order logic problems, yet 87\\% of its proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises.\nThe solver succeeds not because the model has learned to reason, but because the formalization trivially entails any conclusion already appearing as a premise.\nThis structural shortcut (an instance of shortcut learning~\\citep{geirhos2020shortcut} in the neurosymbolic setting) is invisible to execution-based evaluation: prove rate registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation or degenerate circular embedding.\n\n\\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}\nProve rate, the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}, conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.\nRecent work applies GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on component necessity.\nProcess reward models~\\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.\nA separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \\emph{diagnostic} for component necessity in neurosymbolic outputs.\n\n\\paragraph{Component necessity testing.}\nWe address this gap with \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic applying \\emph{component necessity testing}, leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs, to quantify component necessity.\nFor each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \\emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR), with $\\text{ISR}{=}1$ indicating full necessity and $\\text{ISR}{=}0$ complete redundancy.\nLOO ablation itself is standard~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; our contribution is the empirical discovery that the same LOO structure making ISR informative as a diagnostic also makes it vulnerable to gaming as a reward, a compositional Goodhart effect on structural metrics.\nISR captures component necessity rather than semantic faithfulness (downstream NLI: $\\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig5_training_isr_curves.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR training trajectories across reward conditions (proved samples only, rolling $w{=}20$). Exec-only collapses to $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0$, confirming structural degeneration (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); the ${\\sim}0.5$ plateau is an $n{=}2$ artifact. ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds rise toward $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}1$ via TS gaming (Goodhart effect, \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); random remains low.}\n\\label{fig:training-isr-curves}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR as diagnostic.}\nAs a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\\citep{folio}, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR (Figure~\\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}): for seed~42, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{:}\\,0.642\\to 0.132\\to 0.003$ over 100--1000 steps (99.5\\% degradation; all 3 seeds: ${\\leq}7\\%$ non-hacking by 1000 steps) while PR rises $32\\%{\\to}96\\%$.\nThree seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}47$\\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different mode (conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse), yet all reach ${\\geq}93\\%$ structural hacking by 1000 steps.\nAn SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\\text{PR}{=}13.0\\%$ with 92.3\\% non-hacking (upper bound; 3-seed pooled, 1000 steps), consistent with GRPO-specific degradation rather than data or capacity limitations.\n\n\\paragraph{Goodhart effects.}\nRepurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\\text{exec}} \\cdot (1 + \\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$, ISR produces a compositional Goodhart effect~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nOn FOLIO, this instantiates \\emph{regressional} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: $P{\\to}P$ tautologies yield $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, so ISR rewards the shortcut; at $\\beta{=}0.1$, ISR increases TS rates in 3 of 5 seeds (70.6\\% vs.\\ 13.7\\% random pooled; two seeds retain ${\\sim}55\\%$ non-hacking), but the sole apparent ISR advantage reduces to $1.06\\times$ after controlling for premise count.\nAt $\\beta{\\geq}0.2$, \\emph{extremal} Goodhart dominates ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$ ISR, $54{\\pm}35\\%$ random at $\\beta{=}0.3$).\nOn ProofFOL, hacking yields $\\text{ISR}{=}0.0$, so ISR correctly penalizes shortcuts, associated with beneficial instability (76.8\\% vs.\\ 17.4\\% genuine under a coarser classifier, $p{=}0.10$).\nISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \\emph{reliable as observer, dataset-dependent as optimizer.}\n\n\\paragraph{Contributions.}\nIn this case study (Qwen3-4B, 6 reward conditions, 3--5 seeds, 2 logic types, 2 dataset families, up to 1000 steps), we contribute: (\\textbf{C1}) STA as a diagnostic framework (ISR metric, eight-mode hacking taxonomy, and cross-domain evaluation protocol) revealing structural collapse invisible to PR (${\\leq}7\\%$ non-hacking, 3 seeds, 1000 steps; 99.5\\% in seed~42), with an SFT control consistent with GRPO-specific degradation (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\\textbf{C2}) a compositional Goodhart effect on ISR-as-reward: TS amplification without significant prevalence reduction ($p{=}0.182$; apparent ISR advantage collapses to $1.06\\times$ after deconfounding; shuffled-ISR control rules out distributional shape; \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}), with cross-domain evidence: confirmed in Prolog (3/3 seeds 0\\% non-hacking) and exploratory in ProofFOL ($p{=}0.10$), showing dataset-dependent outcomes (\\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}).\nThese are cautionary results from a Qwen3-4B case study, replicated across dataset families, relevant to any work treating a structural proxy as a training signal (generalization scope discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:limitations}).\n",

ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds rise toward $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}1$ via TS gaming (Goodhart effect, \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); random remains low.}\n\\label{fig:training-isr-curves}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR as diagnostic.}\nAs a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\\citep{folio}, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR (Figure~\\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}): for seed~42, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{:}\\,0.642\\to 0.132\\to 0.003$ over 100--1000 steps (99.5\\% degradation; all 3 seeds: ${\\leq}7\\%$ non-hacking by 1000 steps) while PR rises $32\\%{\\to}96\\%$.\nThree seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}47$\\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different mode (conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse), yet all reach ${\\geq}93\\%$ structural hacking by 1000 steps.\nAn SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\\text{PR}{=}13.0\\%$ with 92.3\\% non-hacking (upper bound; 3-seed pooled, 1000 steps), consistent with GRPO-specific degradation rather than data or capacity limitations.\n\n\\paragraph{Goodhart effects.}\nRepurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\\text{exec}} \\cdot (1 + \\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$, ISR produces a compositional Goodhart effect~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nOn FOLIO, this instantiates \\emph{regressional} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: $P{\\to}P$ tautologies yield $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, so ISR rewards the shortcut; at $\\beta{=}0.1$, ISR significantly shifts hacking composition from CE to TS ($p{<}10^{-15}$; 3 of 5 seeds) without significantly reducing prevalence ($p{=}0.182$); the sole apparent ISR advantage reduces to $1.06\\times$ after controlling for premise count.\nAt $\\beta{\\geq}0.2$, \\emph{extremal} Goodhart dominates ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$ ISR, $54{\\pm}35\\%$ random at $\\beta{=}0.3$).\nOn ProofFOL, hacking yields $\\text{ISR}{=}0.0$, so ISR correctly penalizes shortcuts, associated with beneficial instability (76.8\\% vs.\\ 17.4\\% genuine under a coarser classifier, $p{=}0.10$).\nISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \\emph{reliable as observer, dataset-dependent as optimizer.}\n\n\\paragraph{Contributions.}\nIn this case study (Qwen3-4B, 6 reward conditions, 3--5 seeds, 2 logic types, 2 dataset families, up to 1000 steps), we contribute: (\\textbf{C1}) STA as a diagnostic framework (ISR metric, eight-mode hacking taxonomy, and cross-domain evaluation protocol) revealing structural collapse invisible to PR (${\\leq}7\\%$ non-hacking, 3 seeds, 1000 steps; 99.5\\% in seed~42), with an SFT control consistent with GRPO-specific degradation (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\\textbf{C2}) a compositional Goodhart effect on ISR-as-reward: TS amplification without significant prevalence reduction ($p{=}0.182$; apparent ISR advantage collapses to $1.06\\times$ after deconfounding; shuffled-ISR control rules out distributional shape; \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}), with cross-domain evidence: confirmed in Prolog (3/3 seeds 0\\% non-hacking) and exploratory in ProofFOL ($p{=}0.10$), showing dataset-dependent outcomes (\\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}).\nThese are cautionary results from a Qwen3-4B case study, replicated across dataset families, relevant to any work treating a structural proxy as a training signal (generalization scope discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:limitations}).\n",

_source line 2225 · 2026-05-21T17:12:34.490Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0179LYvyLcERv1PLdwECtz4t`</summary>

```text
39	We reverse ISR's role from observer to reward: $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, with matched random control ($u\sim\mathcal{U}(0,1)$).
40	The compositional Goodhart effect operates at the level of ISR's LOO definition: models learn to generate formalizations where each component is individually necessary for proof mechanics, without ensuring meaningful reasoning.
41	ISR decline indicates structural change; the mode breakdown (Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}) characterizes this change as degeneration.
42	This is distinct from simple proxy failure. The qualitative shift in hacking modes (TS $0\%{\to}71\%$ under ISR, per-seed direction consistent) demonstrates active optimization against the LOO metric; deconfounding shows the apparent $3.2\times$ ISR advantage collapses to $1.06\times$ after controlling for premise count.
43	
44	\paragraph{Multiplicative coupling.}
45	The reward $r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1{+}\beta\,\text{ISR})$ couples signals; exec-only's $0\%$ non-hacking, the qualitative TS shift ($0\%{\to}71\%$), and an additive variant's PR collapse (Appendix~\ref{app:setup}) constrain this confound.
46	ISR scores are bimodal (near $0$ or ${\geq}0.5$) while random is approximately uniform. A shuffled-ISR control (batch-internal random permutation preserving ISR's bimodal distribution while breaking the sample-level semantic link) yields $\text{PR}{=}15.3{\pm}0.6\%$ with $87.0\%$ non-hacking ($N{=}46$ proved, 3-seed pooled; per-seed $n{\approx}15$ limits statistical power), comparable to SFT ($\text{PR}{=}13\%$, $92\%$ non-hacking) and far below the TS-dominated ISR regime ($71\%$ TS). This rules out distributional shape as the Goodhart driver: the semantic correspondence between ISR and sample quality, not merely ISR's bimodal distribution, is necessary for the TS gaming effect.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01YBvSLrKuLuzQ4JccR8Jrhm`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation: ISR reveals near-complete collapse under exec-only GRPO (e.g., $214\times$ for seed~42) that PR hides, while SFT results are consistent with GRPO-specific degradation (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
5	Cross-logic replication in Prolog confirms the phenomenon generalizes beyond FOL (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
6	Repurposed as a reward, ISR produces a compositional Goodhart effect: on FOLIO, it shifts hacking from CE to TS in 3 of 5 seeds (supplementary CMH $p{<}10^{-15}$; two seeds retain ${\sim}55\%$ non-hacking) without significantly reducing overall hacking prevalence ($p{=}0.182$); on ProofFOL, beneficial instability yields 76.8\% vs.\ 17.4\% non-hacking (upper bound; \S\ref{sec:setup}) ($p{=}0.10$).
7	Deconfounding reduces the sole apparent ISR reward advantage to $1.06\times$; a shuffled-ISR control confirms that semantic content, not distributional shape, drives the compositional shift (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}).
8	The central finding is ISR's observer/optimizer asymmetry: the same LOO structure that makes ISR informative as a diagnostic makes it gameable as a reward, with outcomes depending on whether the dataset's hacking geometry satisfies the metric.
9	
10	For practitioners in settings similar to ours (medium-scale models with LOO-tractable formalization; \S\ref{sec:limitations}): never evaluate neurosymbolic systems by prove rate alone (use ISR and component count as joint diagnostics with mode-level breakdown), always report multi-seed results, and use ISR for post-hoc diagnosis only since any dose induces gaming or collapse when optimized (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
11	
```

</details>

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "828b084c-d939-4396-b753-8f06120617f5",

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
    "oldString": "\\citet{helff2026gaming} and \\citet{khalifa2026countdown} document verification gaming and provide RLVR hacking testbeds; STA is complementary, offering a structural diagnostic and eight-mode taxonomy for neurosymbolic settings where gaming \\emph{emerges during training}.",
    "newString": "\\citet{helff2026gaming} propose Input Perturbation Testing (IPT), which perturbs verifier inputs to detect gaming; STA shares the perturbation intuition but operates at the component level (LOO ablation on solver premises vs.\\ token-level perturbation on verifier prompts) and targets neurosymbolic settings where gaming \\emph{emerges during training}.\n\\citet{khalifa2026countdown} provide RLVR hacking testbeds.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}\nLLM-solver pipelines~\\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15, deepseek-r1} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for component necessity.\nAlphaProof~\\citep{alphaproof2025} applies RL-based formal verification at scale in Lean~4; STA-style diagnostics could probe whether similar structural degeneration occurs in theorem-proving settings.\nWe use FOLIO~\\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.\n\n\\paragraph{Process rewards and reward shaping.}\nReward shaping theory~\\citep{ng1999policy} establishes conditions under which auxiliary rewards preserve optimal policies; early work applied verifier-based training to mathematical reasoning~\\citep{cobbe2021training}, with process-level feedback showing promise over outcome-only signals~\\citep{uesato2022solving}.\nLearned PRMs~\\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} and verifier-guided search~\\citep{nlproofs, vstar} provide step-level feedback but suffer from reward-model noise and distribution shift~\\citep{processbench}; \\citet{easy-to-hard} show that verifiers trained on easy tasks can generalize to hard ones, though structural gaming remains unaddressed.\n\\citet{gao-effective-rl-reward} show that auxiliary process/outcome rewards can \\emph{hurt} RL training relative to success-only reward, a finding our ISR-as-reward results corroborate; \\citet{setlur2025rewarding} further demonstrate that reward bonus magnitude must be carefully calibrated, echoing our $\\beta$-dependent Goodhart transitions.\nSymbolic feedback approaches~\\citep{rlsf} use solver certificates as token-level rewards, achieving gains on program synthesis; ISR differs in operating at the component level (LOO ablation) rather than the token level, and our results show this structural signal is gamed when optimized.\n\n\\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}\nGoodhart's Law is established theoretically~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RLHF and frontier models~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization, bondarenko-spec-gaming, kim-game-formalization}.\n\\citet{kim-game-formalization} document inference-time gaming in logical formalization on FOLIO using frontier models with Lean~4.\nOur work is complementary: training-time (vs.\\ inference), component necessity (vs.\\ faithfulness), medium-model/Prover9 (vs.\\ frontier/Lean~4); combining STA with inference-time faithfulness detection would provide complete gaming coverage.\n\\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes \\emph{catastrophic} Goodhart where KL regularization provably fails, offering a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent the ISR collapse we observe.\nRecent work further documents spurious reward signals in RLVR~\\citep{spurious-rewards, yan2026spurious}, training instability via objective-level hacking~\\citep{dong2026probing}, reward hacking in frontier models~\\citep{metr-reward-hacking}, and proposes verifiable composite or process rewards to mitigate outcome-only limitations~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}.\n\\citet{helff2026gaming} and \\citet{khalifa2026countdown} document verification gaming and provide RLVR hacking testbeds; STA is complementary, offering a structural diagnostic and eight-mode taxonomy for neurosymbolic settings where gaming \\emph{emerges during training}.\nThe Goodhart effect follows standard taxonomy~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: regressional on FOLIO (tautologies satisfy the LOO metric), extremal at high $\\beta$. Its application to a structural diagnostic designed to \\emph{detect} gaming is, to our knowledge, novel; the dataset-dependent asymmetry (the same metric gamed on one dataset, beneficial on another) is a non-trivial empirical finding not predicted by the taxonomy alone.\n\n\\paragraph{Mutation testing.}\nSTA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; novelty lies in application and findings, not in the perturbation primitive.\n",

          " \\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes \\emph{catastrophic} Goodhart where KL regularization provably fails, offering a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent the ISR collapse we observe.",
          " Recent work further documents spurious reward signals in RLVR~\\citep{spurious-rewards, yan2026spurious}, training instability via objective-level hacking~\\citep{dong2026probing}, reward hacking in frontier models~\\citep{metr-reward-hacking}, and proposes verifiable composite or process rewards to mitigate outcome-only limitations~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}.",
          "-\\citet{helff2026gaming} and \\citet{khalifa2026countdown} document verification gaming and provide RLVR hacking testbeds; STA is complementary, offering a structural diagnostic and eight-mode taxonomy for neurosymbolic settings where gaming \\emph{emerges during training}.",
